- **A named pipe (`syscall.Mkfifo`), not a Unix domain socket, stands in for "some irregular file type."** An earlier probe with `net.Listen("unix", sockPath)` passed, but the resulting socket path (`t.TempDir()` plus a filename) sits uncomfortably close to macOS's ~104-byte `sun_path` limit — a longer test name or deeper temp-dir nesting could silently flip the probe to an unrelated "invalid argument" failure instead of proving the intended behavior. `syscall.Mkfifo` has no such length ceiling and lands in the identical `default:` arm. Before committing to `syscall.Mkfifo`'s POSIX-only nature (absent on Windows), confirmed — rather than assumed — that this repository's CI never runs `go vet`/`go test` on a Windows runner: `release.yml`'s `windows-latest` matrix entry only runs `wails build`, which compiles the main package but not `_test.go` files, and every job that does run `go vet`/`go test` (`ci.yml`) is `ubuntu-24.04`/`ubuntu-latest`. If a Windows test/vet job is ever added, this specific test will need a `//go:build !windows` guard — noted in the test's own doc comment for whoever adds that job.
- **`MigrateLegacyRoot`'s own early-return guard is masked by `migrateLegacyRoot`'s identical duplicate check one call deeper — the `internal/admin` redundant-downstream-guard shape (the `LogDocumentSignatureOutcome` finding) recurring for a third time this session.** Confirmed by direct mutation before writing the real test: deleting `MigrateLegacyRoot`'s own `os.Stat(newRoot/system.db)` check produces the exact same `(false, nil)` result, because `migrateLegacyRoot` repeats the identical check one call deeper (it needs its own copy regardless, since it's also invoked once per legacy candidate). `TestMigrateLegacyRoot_NewRootAlreadyInitialised` is kept as a coverage test with a docstring that says exactly this — the outer check is a pure optimization (skips `legacyRootCandidates()` and a redundant `os.Stat` per candidate), not a second correctness layer — rather than a false guard-presence claim. `TestMigrateLegacyRoot_NoLegacyInstallFound` (the loop-exhausted fallback) is coverage-only for an unrelated reason: that specific `return false, nil` statement can't be deleted without a "missing return" compile error, so there's no mutation to run against it at all.
- **The one genuine break-verification test in that trio, `TestMigrateLegacyRoot_PropagatesCopyTreeError`, forces `migrateLegacyRoot`'s `copyTree` call to fail** (a `newRoot` subdirectory pre-occupied by a plain file, using `t.Setenv("XDG_DATA_HOME", ...)` — validated in the prior sub-increment's planning — to make `legacyRootCandidates()`'s real candidate-resolution path test-controllable) and confirms `MigrateLegacyRoot` propagates the error out through its loop instead of swallowing it. Break-verified by removing the propagation (`migrated, _ := migrateLegacyRoot(...)` instead of checking `err`) and confirming the test goes red.
- **`MigrateLegacyRoot`, `migrateLegacyRoot`, and `copyFile` all reach 100%.** Two branches stay disclosed-untested in `copyTree`: `filepath.Rel`'s error (unforceable — `filepath.WalkDir` always constructs `path` from `src` via `filepath.Join`, so the two are always `Rel`-compatible by construction) and `d.Info()`'s error (a mid-walk TOCTOU race between the directory listing and the stat call, with no deterministic, portable trigger).
- **`internal/users` is now closed out entirely** — every remaining uncovered line across `dek.go` (90.2%), `recovery.go` (92.9%), and `store.go` (93.6%) traces to a named, disclosed reason; package total 93.0%. Six commits total across the package's full sweep this session: `3668623` (`dek.go`), `f0f6577` (`recovery.go` increment 1), `634e788` (`recovery.go` increment 2), `6a35bb6` (`store.go` sub-increment 1), `3aef640` (same-day correction), and this one (`store.go` sub-increment 2).
- **Follow-up self-critique before declaring the closeout claim true: verified `dek.go` and `recovery.go`'s disclosures against the current coverage profile, not just trusted the prior increments' prose.** Re-ran the `@@UNCOV@@` HTML extraction for both files and matched each uncovered line to an in-code comment by hand. Found one real gap: `recovery.go`'s `IssueRecoveryCodes` `tx.Begin()` check (line 104) was disclosed in this session's ledger/handbook prose from an earlier increment ("preceded by an earlier DB call a closed-DB fault trips first") but had no matching in-code comment — the same invariant break just fixed for `copyTree`'s two branches, recurring in a file from a prior increment rather than the one just written. Added the comment; comment-only change, coverage unchanged at 93.0%. Every other uncovered line in `dek.go` (2 lines: `migrateDEKColumns`' PRAGMA `Scan`/`rows.Err()`) and `recovery.go` (6 lines: `IssueRecoveryCodes`' now-commented `tx.Begin()` and its `tx.Commit()`, `ResetWithRecoveryCode`'s `rows.Scan`/`rows.Err()`, `RemainingRecoveryCodes`' `sql.ErrNoRows`, `generateCode`'s length check) now has a matching in-code comment — the closeout claim is verified, not assumed.
- **Corrected the ledger's own "two prior numeric slips" to "three"** — it undercounted itself: the 92.2%-vs-92.9% file/function figure, the `-run`-pattern test-count methodology (correct answer, unreliable method), and the eleven-vs-twelve disclosed-branch enumeration this same entry just fixed. A fourth reminder that every unverified numeric claim in these documents has needed at least one correction — hence the standing shift to enumerating rather than counting wherever a list is doing the real work.
- **Coverage ratchet updated:** go_default 8755/14627 (5872 uncovered) → 8767/14627 (5860 uncovered); go_duckdb 8833/14725 (5892 uncovered) → 8845/14725 (5880 uncovered). frontend unchanged.

### 2026-08-05 — `internal/pdfmeta` (increment 1): 81.7% → 86.6%; the first package this session where a bare `bytes.Contains` on rendered PDF output was itself the masking risk, not a closed-DB/rand.Reader cascade

- **First package targeted after `internal/users`' full closeout.** Surveyed every completion-tier package (70–99%) with a per-package uncovered-function count: `pdfmeta` had 27 partially-covered functions, the largest concentration, ahead of `fonts`/`rfc3161`/`signing` (~81.6% each) and `sigma/service` (85.0%). Scoped increment 1 to the file's two pure, fixture-free outliers — `ApplyPDFAMetadata` (0%, a real production helper wired into `internal/documents` and `internal/export`, just never unit-tested) and `insertOutputIntentsReference` (30.3%, pure byte manipulation, no error paths) — leaving the file's larger byte-surgery cluster (`parseTrailerSizeAndRoot`, `InjectPAdESSignature`, etc., all needing malformed-PDF fixtures) for a later increment, the same file-then-function splitting discipline established for `internal/users`.
- **New forcing/verification technique for this package: inspecting actual rendered PDF bytes, since `fpdf.Fpdf` exposes no getters for `/Info` dict fields (`Title`/`Author`/`Subject`/`Keywords`/`Creator` are all private).** Read `go-pdf/fpdf@v0.9.0`'s source before relying on it, not assumed: `SetTitle`/`SetAuthor`/etc. with `isUTF8=true` store the value as UTF-16BE with a leading BOM, and `putinfo()` writes each field as `"/Key (" + <those bytes> + ")"` with the value immediately adjacent to the key. That adjacency is what makes the technique reliable — a bare `bytes.Contains(output, utf16be(want))` would match if `want` happened to appear as some *other* field's value, so every assertion uses the key-prefixed needle `"/Key (" + utf16be(want)`.
- **The adjacency technique wasn't optional — a real masking risk it prevented, caught by predicting the mutation before writing the assertion.** `TestApplyPDFAMetadata_DefaultsAuthorWhenEmpty`'s natural first-draft assertion (`bytes.Contains(output, utf16be("GoPMgr"))`) would have been masked: `pdf.SetCreator("GoPMgr", true)` runs unconditionally three lines after the author-default guard, so "GoPMgr" (UTF-16-encoded) is in the output regardless of whether the guard ran. Confirmed by direct mutation (deleting `if spec.Author == "" { spec.Author = "GoPMgr" }`) before finalizing the fix: the key-adjacent needle goes red under that mutation, a bare-Contains check would not have.
- **`insertOutputIntentsReference`'s "value already exists" branch, if deleted, doesn't fail obviously — it produces a duplicate key.** The function has three mutually-exclusive outcomes (malformed body → wrap; existing `/OutputIntents` → replace; absent → insert fresh), and deleting the middle branch's guard doesn't remove `/OutputIntents` from the output — execution falls through to the "insert fresh" branch and adds a **second** `/OutputIntents` entry while the stale one survives untouched. Confirmed by direct mutation (short-circuiting the branch's `idx >= 0` check to always-false) before writing the real assertion: a bare `bytes.Contains(result, []byte("/OutputIntents"))` stays green under that mutation (there are now two matches instead of zero), so all three "value already exists" tests assert `bytes.Count(result, []byte("/OutputIntents")) == 1` instead.
- **Two covered guards break-verify via panic, not differing error text — a new verification mechanism for this package (and this session), not a disclosed-untested branch.** `ApplyPDFAMetadata`'s `pdf == nil` check and its `len(spec.Keywords) > 0` guard (protecting `spec.Keywords[0]` on what would otherwise be an empty-slice index) both crash the caller if deleted, rather than returning a different value. `TestApplyPDFAMetadata_NilPDFIsNoop` exists to pin the guard's presence via the ABSENCE of a crash, not via any return-value comparison; the keywords guard is exercised by `TestApplyPDFAMetadata_DefaultsAuthorWhenEmpty`, `TestApplyPDFAMetadata_OmitsTitleAndSubjectWhenEmpty`, and `TestApplyPDFAMetadata_DefaultsCreatorToolWhenEmpty` — the tests that pass an empty `Keywords` slice past the nil-pdf guard (`NilPDFIsNoop` itself never reaches it; it returns at the nil check first). Both mutations were run directly (not just predicted) before this entry was written: deleting the nil check panics inside fpdf's `SetTitle` with a nil-pointer dereference, and unwrapping the keywords guard panics with index-out-of-range on `spec.Keywords[0]`; both restores were `diff`-confirmed clean against the pre-mutation backup.
- **`TestInsertOutputIntentsReference_InsertsFreshEntry` is coverage-only, not break-verifiable** — same reasoning as `internal/users`' `MigrateLegacyRoot` loop-exhausted fallback (`3aef640`'s entry above): it's the function's final statement with nothing following it, so deleting it is a "missing return" compile error, not a behavior change.
- **`icc.go`'s `DefaultICCProfile`'s `len(sRGBICC) == 0` branch stays disclosed-untested, confirmed rather than assumed to be the `//go:embed`-at-compile-time category:** `sRGBICC` is populated from `assets/sRGB.icc`, a file checked into the repo, so the branch is unreachable in any build that actually compiles — forcing it would mean compiling a different variant of the package, not writing a test.
- **A metadata inconsistency surfaced in passing, deliberately not fixed as part of this coverage task:** `ApplyPDFAMetadata` hardcoded the `/Info` dict's Creator field to `"GoPMgr"` and never read `XMPSpec.CreatorTool`, while `BuildXMPPacket` (this same file, a few hundred lines up) DID honor `CreatorTool` for the XMP packet's own `<xmp:CreatorTool>` element — confirmed by grep before writing the "all fields" test, not assumed. A caller that set `CreatorTool` got inconsistent metadata between the two surfaces GoPMgr writes into the same PDF. Recorded in the test's docstring and the ledger as an observed, unverified-as-intentional divergence rather than silently pinned as correct behavior; out of scope for this particular increment since it's a coverage task, not a bug-fix task. **Resolved same-day — see the entry immediately below.**
- **Coverage ratchet updated:** go_default 8767/14627 (5860 uncovered) → 8805/14627 (5822 uncovered); go_duckdb 8845/14725 (5880 uncovered) → 8883/14725 (5842 uncovered). frontend unchanged.

### 2026-08-05 — resolved the `ApplyPDFAMetadata`/`BuildXMPPacket` `CreatorTool` inconsistency; removed three dead re-export shims from `internal/export/pdfa.go`

- **This is a consistency alignment, not a bug fix — say so precisely.** `/advisor`'s plan review pushed back hard on framing this as "found and fixed a bug": `pdfmeta.go`'s own comment documented the hardcoding as deliberate ("the export package overrides with the real version string when it re-exports"), and the blast-radius check below shows zero observable behavior change on any current call site. The correct framing is that `ApplyPDFAMetadata` now honors `spec.CreatorTool` the same way its sibling `BuildXMPPacket` already does — defaulting to `"GoPMgr"` when unset, otherwise using the caller's value — bringing the two metadata surfaces (`/Info` dict and XMP packet) into agreement when built from the same spec.
- **Confirmed, not assumed, that the fix changes nothing for any live caller.** Grepped every `.ApplyPDFAMetadata(` call site in the module before writing the fix: `internal/documents/fonts.go`'s only direct call already passes `CreatorTool: "GoPMgr"`, identical to the prior hardcoded value — no change. `internal/export`'s `ApplyPDFAMetadata` wrapper used to unconditionally override Creator with `"GoPMgr " + exportVersion()` immediately after delegating to `pdfmeta.ApplyPDFAMetadata` — but grepping its own call sites (`grep -rn "\.ApplyPDFAMetadata(" --include="*.go" .`) turned up exactly two matches: the function's own definition and its internal delegate call. Nothing in the module ever called it. 0% coverage, no test file references it.
- **Found two more dead shims by the same check, one on a second `/advisor` pass after the first draft got it wrong.** `internal/export/pdfa.go`'s `InjectPAdESSignature` re-export had the same shape — `grep -rn "InjectPAdESSignature"` across the module shows the real caller, `internal/signing/pades.go:127`, calls `pdfmeta.InjectPAdESSignature` directly, never the `export` package's wrapper — found and removed on the first pass. The first draft of this entry then claimed the file's remaining shims (`BuildXMPPacket`, `InjectXMPStream`, `MakePDFA3`, `DefaultICCProfile`, `HasDefaultICC`) were "checked the same way and confirmed live," but `/advisor`'s second review caught that `HasDefaultICC` had not actually been checked — re-running `grep -rn "[^.a-zA-Z]HasDefaultICC(" --include="*.go" internal/ | grep -v pdfa.go` found only the `pdfmeta` package's own definition and its own test, no unqualified caller: `internal/export/sigma_report.go:52` calls `pdfmeta.HasDefaultICC()` directly, bypassing the shim entirely, same as the other two. 0% coverage, no tests — identical profile. Removed. `BuildXMPPacket`, `InjectXMPStream`, `MakePDFA3`, and `DefaultICCProfile` each have a genuine unqualified caller inside the package (`pdf.go`, `sigma_report.go`, `montecarlo_report.go`) and stay. Lesson for future dead-code sweeps in this codebase: verify every item in a list individually before writing "confirmed live" — a sweep that checks N-1 of N items and asserts N is exactly the kind of unverified claim this session's process exists to catch.
- **Removing the dead wrapper is what actually resolves the divergence `/advisor` asked about, not the `pdfmeta.go` edit alone.** Before removal, the export path (had it ever been reached) would still have set Creator to a version-suffixed string regardless of `spec.CreatorTool`, leaving one surface unreconciled. After removal there is no live code path left that sets `/Info` Creator to anything other than what `spec.CreatorTool` (or its default) specifies — the question dissolves because the divergent path was unreachable, not because it was patched to agree.
- **Two new tests, each break-verified independently of the other.** `TestApplyPDFAMetadata_HonorsCreatorTool` (non-empty `CreatorTool` reaches `/Creator`) and `TestApplyPDFAMetadata_DefaultsCreatorToolWhenEmpty` (empty `CreatorTool` defaults to `"GoPMgr"`, mirroring `TestApplyPDFAMetadata_DefaultsAuthorWhenEmpty`'s masking risk — a bare `bytes.Contains(b, utf16be("GoPMgr"))` would pass regardless of this guard since `spec.Author` also defaults to `"GoPMgr"` a few lines earlier, so the assertion uses the key-adjacent `"/Creator (" + value` needle). Reverting to the hardcoded `"GoPMgr"` value fails `HonorsCreatorTool` but not `DefaultsCreatorToolWhenEmpty` (the default value is unchanged by that specific mutation); making `CreatorTool` unconditional (dropping the empty-string default) fails `DefaultsCreatorToolWhenEmpty` but not `HonorsCreatorTool`. Both mutations were run and restored (`diff`-confirmed clean) before finalizing.
- **`TestApplyPDFAMetadata_SetsAllFieldsWhenProvided`'s stale comment corrected.** It previously documented the (now-fixed) inconsistency as an open finding; updated to set and assert a non-default `CreatorTool` value alongside the other fields, with a pointer to `TestApplyPDFAMetadata_HonorsCreatorTool` for the guard-specific break-verification.
- **Coverage ratchet updated:** go_default 8805/14627 (5822 uncovered) → 8807/14624 (5817 uncovered); go_duckdb 8883/14725 (5842 uncovered) → 8885/14722 (5837 uncovered). Decomposition, corrected on `/advisor`'s second pass (the first draft's "uncovered drops by 4" arithmetic was wrong): the three removed shims total 5 statements — `ApplyPDFAMetadata`'s body (delegate call, `if pdf != nil` guard, `SetCreator` call — 3 statements) plus `InjectPAdESSignature` and `HasDefaultICC` (1 each) — all previously uncovered, so removing them alone accounts for the full −5 uncovered delta on both tags. The `pdfmeta.go` fix separately added 2 new statements (the `if spec.CreatorTool == ""` guard and its assignment), both immediately covered by the two new tests; they show up in the +2 covered delta and the denominator arithmetic (−5 removed +2 added = −3 total) but contribute nothing to the uncovered delta, since a statement that's covered the moment it's added was never counted as uncovered. frontend unchanged. `go build ./...`, `go vet`, and the full test suite for `internal/pdfmeta`, `internal/export`, `internal/documents`, and `internal/signing` (both build tags) all pass.

### 2026-08-05 — coverage ratchet exclude-filter flake: intermittent ~3× inflated statement counts, spurious REGRESSED (tracked, not yet fixed)

- **Observed six times this session, every time during a `make coverage-ratchet-update` run whose real change was tiny.** First occurrence: after removing three dead 1-statement shims from `internal/export/pdfa.go`, the ratchet reported go_default 22404/36617 instead of the expected ~8807/14624 — roughly 2.5× inflated on both covered and total, spuriously flagged REGRESSED. Second occurrence: during increment 2a (a pure test-file addition, zero production-code change), the same pattern recurred at go_default 25088/42320 vs. the expected 8818/14624 — roughly 3× inflated. Third occurrence: during increment 2b's own `--update` run. Fourth occurrence: on a later same-session re-verification of that same 2b state (go_default 25103/42320 vs. the expected 8833/14624). Fifth occurrence: during increment 3a's `--update` run. Sixth occurrence: during increment 4a's `--update` run (go_default 18239/27920 vs. the expected 8892/14639 — again roughly 2× inflated) — confirming the flake isn't tied to any particular commit's diff shape, just to running the ratchet script at all. Every occurrence resolved on an immediate identical re-run.
- **Suspected root cause, not yet confirmed by a fix:** `scripts/coverage-go.sh`'s exclude-filter step is `tail -n +2 "$profile" | grep -Ev -f <(grep -Ev '^\s*(#|$)' "$exclude_file") || true` — process substitution (`<(...)`) feeding `grep -f`. Under macOS's shipped bash 3.2, this can race: if the process substitution's pipe isn't fully populated when `grep -f` opens it, `grep -f` may read an empty pattern file, and `grep -Ev` with an empty pattern set passes every line through unfiltered — inflating the statement count by however much `scripts/coverage-exclude-go.txt` would otherwise have excluded.
- **Confirmed directionally, not conclusively:** ran a fully unfiltered profile (`go test -coverprofile=... $(go list ./... | grep -v node_modules)`, no exclude step, no `-tags duckdb`) and got 26421/43881 — the same order of magnitude as both anomalous runs, consistent with (but not proof of) the exclude-filter-failed hypothesis.
- **Handling in the meantime: never accept a REGRESSED result without re-running once.** All six of this session's occurrences resolved on an immediate identical re-run. `--update` never writes a worse mark than the stored baseline regardless (see `scripts/coverage-ratchet.sh`'s own comment on this), so a bad run can't silently corrupt `coverage-baseline.json` — but it can waste time debugging a phantom regression if taken at face value.
- **Not fixed here — flagged as a background task** (spawned via the session's task-chip mechanism, not yet actioned) with a specific proposed fix: replace the process substitution with a real temp file fully written before `grep -f` reads it, and verify by running `make coverage-ratchet-update` in a loop (10-20 iterations) confirming identical, stable output every time — the actual regression test for a flake that doesn't reproduce reliably on a single run.

### 2026-08-05 — `internal/pdfmeta` increment 2a: 86.6% → 88.0%; the leaf parsers in the `InjectXMPStream` call chain, split from 2b per `/advisor`'s plan review, three masking risks caught by running every mutation rather than predicting it

- **Increment 2 was originally planned as 8 functions / ~20 tests; `/advisor`'s pre-implementation plan review split it into 2a and 2b before any code was written.** The original scope mixed two techniques with different risk profiles: pure byte-literal unit tests for the leaf parsers (no staging needed) and staged-fixture tests for the call chain itself (`InjectXMPStream`'s three error-wrapping branches, which need PDF-shaped inputs crafted to fail at exactly one stage — the same wrapping-chain masking risk this package's increment-1 planning already flagged for a different function). Same reasoning as the `internal/admin` review-load lesson earlier this session (`internal/admin` was covered in one large pass; the masked-mutation finding there was still caught, but the review load made it harder than it needed to be, and every increment since has split by function or technique to keep each pass's review load bounded): two review-risk profiles in one increment is two reviews' worth of exposure in one pass, harder to catch either kind of mistake carefully. 2a took the low-risk half: `findLastStartxref`, `readDictInt`, `readDictRef`, `findObjectBody`, `insertMetadataReference` — 11 statements, all now 100%. `pdfmeta.go`'s production code is untouched; confirmed via `git status --short` / `git diff --stat` before committing, not assumed.
- **Masking risk #1 (fixture-dependent): `readDictInt`'s and `readDictRef`'s "key not present" guards.** Deleting `if idx < 0 { return ... }` leaves `idx == -1`, and the subsequent `i := idx + len(key)` arithmetic can coincidentally land `i` on a byte position that's non-digit for an unrelated reason — tripping the *next* guard ("no integer value" / "no id digit") instead of falling through to success. Both first-draft tests used a bare `err == nil` check, which stayed green under the mutation (the function still returned *some* error, just the wrong one). Caught by actually running the mutation, not predicted: the test's own docstring was written *after* the mutation confirmed the exact masking error text ("`/Size: no integer value`" instead of "`/Size not present`"). Fixed by asserting the guard's own error-text substring on both.
- **Masking risk #2 (structurally unconditional, a new category): `readDictRef`'s "no id digit" guard.** This is categorically different from every other cascade found this session (the Author/Creator masking, the `/OutputIntents` duplicate-key cascade, `insertMetadataReference`'s own trim-loop masking below) — all of those were fixture- or context-dependent and could in principle have been avoided by choosing a different input. This one cannot be avoided by any input: the leading whitespace-skip immediately before the id-digit scan already consumes every space, tab, newline, and CR, so by the time the id-digit scan starts, the byte it's looking at is guaranteed to be non-whitespace. If that byte is also non-digit (which is exactly the condition that fires "no id digit"), the *second* whitespace-skip (space/tab only, right before the gen-digit scan) can't advance past it either — it's non-whitespace by the same argument. So the gen-digit scan starts at the identical byte position and fails for the identical reason, for every possible input. There is no fixture that dodges this; the only available discriminator is asserting the specific error-text substring, which is what the fix does. Worth naming as its own category in future increments: "structurally masked by an adjacent guard with provably identical failure conditions" is a stronger and more permanent kind of masking than "this particular fixture happens to trigger the next guard too."
- **Masking risk #3 (fixture-dependent, same technique as increment 1): `insertMetadataReference`'s leading-whitespace-trim test.** The function's other tests all pass a catalog body starting with `<<` directly, so the trim loop's body (stripping leading whitespace) was never exercised. The natural test — a body with leading whitespace, asserting the result contains the expected `/Metadata` and `/Pages` substrings — passed even with the trim loop deleted: an untrimmed body starts with a whitespace byte instead of `<<`, which trips the function's own "malformed input" guard a few lines later, and *that* guard's fallback path wraps the entire (still-untrimmed) body in a fresh `<<...>>` shell — a result that still contains both expected substrings via simple concatenation. Discriminated using the exact same technique as increment 1's `insertOutputIntentsReference` duplicate-key fix: `bytes.Count(got, []byte("<<")) == 1` — the correct in-place-edit path reuses the original dict's single `<<` unchanged, while the fallback-wrap path nests a *second* `<<` inside it.
- **`TestFindObjectBody_EndobjNotFound` break-verifies via panic, the same category increment 1 named for `ApplyPDFAMetadata`'s guards.** Deleting the "endobj not found" guard leaves `endIdx == -1` from `bytes.Index`, and the subsequent `b[bodyStart : bodyStart+endIdx]` slice expression panics with a slice-bounds-out-of-range error rather than returning a differing value — Go's `testing` package still reports this as a `FAIL`, so the test correctly catches the mutation, just via a crash rather than an assertion mismatch.
- **Two discriminating claims independently re-verified after `/advisor`'s adversarial pass caught that the first draft asserted rather than demonstrated them.** `TestFindLastStartxref_OffsetOutOfRange`'s docstring claimed its table-driven test covers "offset <= 0" and "offset >= len(b)" independently, but the original mutation deleted the *entire* guard, which doesn't prove either subcase discriminates its own operand specifically. Verified with two follow-up half-mutations (keeping only `offset >= len(b)`, then only `offset <= 0`): each correctly failed only its own subcase, confirming the claim was true — but it had been asserted, not shown, before the check. Separately, `TestInsertMetadataReference_WrapsNonDictBody`'s docstring quoted a stale `/Metadata 99 0 R` literal left over from an earlier draft that used `metaID = 99`; the actual test uses `metaID = 42` (matching its mutation-confirmed output `"/T\n/Metadata 42 0 Rype /Catalog"`) — the same class of numeric slip this ledger has repeatedly needed to correct this session, caught here on the first review pass rather than a follow-up.
- **Coverage ratchet updated:** go_default 8807/14624 (5817 uncovered) → 8818/14624 (5806 uncovered); go_duckdb 8885/14722 (5837 uncovered) → 8896/14722 (5826 uncovered) — 11 newly-covered statements, denominator flat on both tags (test-only increment, no production statements added or removed), matching the pre-implementation prediction exactly. The known exclude-filter flake (see the entry immediately above) reproduced once during this increment's `--update` run; not accepted, re-run instead per that entry's documented handling. `go build ./...`, `gofmt -l`, `go vet`, and the full test suite for `internal/pdfmeta` (both build tags) pass; 53 tests total, confirmed via `grep -c "^func Test"`.

### 2026-08-06 — `internal/pdfmeta` increment 2b: 88.0% → 89.9%; closes the `InjectXMPStream` call chain (`parseTrailerSizeAndRoot`, `InjectXMPStream`, `BuildXMPPacket`); one invalid break-verification mutation caught and corrected by `/advisor`, one self-identified mutation-testing gap closed before finalizing

- **Closes the three functions 2a's plan review deliberately deferred.** All three now 100%; test-only change — `pdfmeta.go`'s production code untouched, confirmed via `git diff --stat` before every commit in this increment. 13 new tests, package total 53 → 66. Full narrative (per-guard masking analysis, mutation outputs, fixture bugs) lives in the ROADMAP entry of the same date to avoid duplicating it here; this entry covers what a coverage-percentage summary wouldn't show — the two review-caught corrections.
- **Statement-count estimate was wrong, and the reason why is the reusable lesson.** 2a's "Not started" pointer estimated 2b at 12 statements; the actual work covered 15. Two causes, both scoping mistakes rather than implementation mistakes: (1) `InjectXMPStream`'s xref-entry-swap block is one `if` branch but three individual assignment statements inside it (`first, second = ...`; `firstOff, secondOff = ...`; `firstGen, secondGen = ...`), each counted separately by Go's coverage instrumentation — eyeballing "one `@@UNCOV@@` span = one statement" undercounts any span with multiple assignments. (2) `BuildXMPPacket`'s `spec.Description != ""` guard wasn't in the original plan at all; it surfaced only after a coverage check post-Title/Author-tests showed 97.0%, not the assumed 100%. Going forward: derive statement-count estimates from `go tool cover -func`'s own numbers before writing them into a "Not started" pointer, not from counting HTML spans by eye.
- **A first-draft break-verification mutation for `parseTrailerSizeAndRoot`'s `/Size` wrap was invalid, caught on `/advisor` review, not self-caught.** The original mutation replaced the guard's fallthrough in a way that let a later `readDictRef` assignment silently overwrite `err` — since the test fixture's `/Root` was valid, `err` ended up `nil` at the check point, and the test went red on `err == nil`, which looks like a pass but actually proves nothing about the `/Size` wrap prefix specifically (it also silently left `size` at its zero value, uninspected). `/advisor` flagged this as "the red is for the wrong reason." Corrected mutation strips only the wrap text — `return 0, 0, 0, err` in place of `return 0, 0, 0, fmt.Errorf("/Size: %w", err)` — preserving the guard's own control flow; re-run confirmed the test correctly goes red on the missing `/Size:` prefix (bare `"not present"` text), with the same fix pattern applied to the `/Root` wrap test.
- **`TestParseTrailerSizeAndRoot_XrefOffsetOutOfRange`'s table was a `map[string]int`, also caught on `/advisor` review.** Go randomizes map iteration order; since the guard's "past len(b)" subcase panics (a genuine slice-bounds violation once mutated) and the panic aborts the whole test function, a map-backed table means which of the other two subcases actually ran before the panic would vary nondeterministically across CI runs — silently reducing effective coverage on some runs without any visible failure. Converted to a slice of `{name, offset}` structs for deterministic execution order; the full-guard mutation was then re-run and confirmed the specific claim already in the docstring: "exactly len(b)" cascades cleanly into a different, still-non-nil error ("trailer keyword not found after xref" — `b[len(b):]` is a legal empty Go slice, so no panic), while "past len(b)" panics on an actual out-of-bounds slice. Both behaviors observed directly under the mutation, not predicted from reading the guard.
- **Gap self-identified before finalizing documentation: the 3 `BuildXMPPacket` tests were not mutation-tested in the increment's first pass.** They were written, confirmed to pass on the unmutated code, and left there — a violation of this session's own repeatedly-enforced rule that "confirmed by mutation" language in a docstring or ledger entry requires the mutation to have actually been run. Caught and closed before writing any documentation claiming otherwise: all three (`DefaultsTitleWhenEmpty`, `DefaultsAuthorWhenEmpty`, `IncludesDescriptionWhenSet`) were subsequently mutation-tested via the standard backup/mutate/run/restore/diff cycle. The Author test's mutation is the interesting one: the guard-deleted output left `<dc:creator>` genuinely empty (`<rdf:li></rdf:li>`) while the three unconditional "GoPMgr" occurrences elsewhere in the packet (`x:xmptk`, `pdf:Producer`, and — since CreatorTool is also unset in this fixture — `xmp:CreatorTool`) remained exactly as predicted, confirming the tag-adjacent needle is the only assertion shape that would have caught this mutation; a bare `bytes.Contains(pkt, []byte("GoPMgr"))` would have stayed green throughout.
- **Two self-authored fixtures accidentally contained the literal keyword they existed to test the absence of** — `"no trailer keyword anywhere in this buffer"` contains "trailer"; `"no startxref marker here"` contains "startxref" — producing false failures on the very first (unmutated) run, not from a mutation. A reminder that filler text in a negative-case fixture needs the same scrutiny as the fixture's structure; both reworded to neutral text before finalizing.
- **Coverage ratchet updated:** go_default 8818/14624 (5806 uncovered) → 8833/14624 (5791 uncovered); go_duckdb 8896/14722 (5826 uncovered) → 8911/14722 (5811 uncovered) — 15 newly-covered statements on both tags, matching the per-function delta above exactly, denominator flat (test-only change). The exclude-filter flake (see the dedicated entry above, now updated to 4 occurrences) reproduced twice across this increment's work. `go build ./...`, `gofmt -l`, `go vet`, and the full `go test ./...` module suite pass; `internal/pdfmeta` at 66 tests, confirmed via `grep -c "^func Test"`.

### 2026-08-06 — the self-negating-fixture mistake recurred a third time: add a mechanical check, not another prose warning

- **A filler-text fixture accidentally containing the literal keyword it exists to test the absence of has now happened three times this session** — twice in increment 2b (`"no trailer keyword anywhere in this buffer"` contains "trailer"; `"no startxref marker here"` contains "startxref"), and again in increment 3a's first draft of `TestEnsureBinaryHeaderComment_PropagatesFindLastStartxrefError` (`"no startxref here at all"` contains "startxref"). All three were caught immediately — the test fails loud on the very first, unmutated run, since the function finds the keyword exactly where the fixture claims it's absent — so none of these ever reached a commit. But three-for-three means the documented lesson from 2b ("filler text needs the same scrutiny as structure") isn't actually preventing the mistake; it's prose that gets read once and not reapplied.
- **Mechanical check going forward, not another prose reminder:** before finalizing any negative-case fixture whose docstring or name claims a keyword is "absent" or "not present," grep the fixture's literal string for that keyword before running the test. This is a 5-second check that would have caught all three occurrences at write-time instead of relying on the test failing loud to catch it — which it did every time, but only after already writing the wrong fixture.

### 2026-08-06 — `internal/pdfmeta` increment 3a: 89.9% → 92.2%; `MakePDFA3`'s binary-header-comment cluster, plus a real silent-corruption bug found and fixed while covering it

- **Covers the subsystem `MakePDFA3` calls first:** `ensureBinaryHeaderComment`, `hasBinaryHeaderComment`, `shiftClassicXrefOffsets`, `parsePositiveDecimal`, `replaceStartxrefValue` — inserting a 5-byte binary marker comment after the PDF header and rewriting every existing classic-xref-table offset plus the trailer's `startxref` pointer to account for the shift. Selected over `MakePDFA3`'s own error wraps and `readTrailerIDValue` (both deferred to a later increment) because this cluster performs the actual byte-offset arithmetic — a bug here is silent file corruption, not a missing test. All target functions now 100% except `ensureBinaryHeaderComment` (95.7%, one disclosed gap — see ROADMAP for the unreachability proof). 19 new tests, package 66 → 85. Full masking-risk and mutation narrative lives in the ROADMAP entry of the same date; this entry covers the two things a coverage-percentage summary alone wouldn't show — the bug found and the test-design correction.
- **Found a real silent-data-corruption bug while writing coverage for existing code, not just added tests to it — flagged by `/advisor`'s pre-implementation plan review, not self-discovered.** `shiftClassicXrefOffsets` rewrote each xref entry's 10-digit offset field via `copy(pdfBytes[entryStart:entryStart+10], []byte(fmt.Sprintf("%010d", oldOff+delta)))`. Go's `copy()` copies `min(len(dst), len(src))` bytes starting from the source's beginning — so if the shifted offset ever needed 11+ digits (the classic xref format's field is a fixed 10), the write would silently truncate instead of erroring. Confirmed directly: `copy()` of the 11-digit string `"10000000004"` into a 10-byte destination yields `"1000000000"` — nearly 10x smaller than correct, no error raised. Only reachable in practice on a PDF whose existing offset is within `delta` (typically 6) of 9999999999 (~9.3GB) — astronomically unlikely for this application's generated reports and charts, but the fix costs three lines: check the shifted value against the field's max before writing, return an error instead of truncating. `TestShiftClassicXrefOffsets_OffsetOverflowReturnsError` break-verifies the new guard directly — no multi-gigabyte fixture needed, since the function only parses and rewrites digit text and never dereferences `pdfBytes` at the parsed offset.
- **A planned test design turned out not to prove what it claimed, caught before finalizing, not after.** The direct positive-path test for `ensureBinaryHeaderComment` was originally planned to round-trip the shifted output through `findLastStartxref`/`parseTrailerSizeAndRoot`/`findObjectBody` (the same shape the existing `TestMakePDFA3PreservesMetadataAndOutputIntentInLatestCatalog` uses) and treat a successful Catalog lookup as proof the offset shift is byte-correct. It isn't: `findObjectBody` locates objects via a textual `"<id> <gen> obj"` scan, never by dereferencing the xref table's recorded offsets, so it reports success even if every offset in the table is wrong, as long as the object text is still present somewhere in the buffer. Confirmed by deliberately mutating the shift's delta by one byte: the `findObjectBody`-based round-trip stayed silently green under that exact mutation. Redesigned to parse the shifted xref entry's own offset field directly and assert `pdfBytes` at that precise offset starts with `"1 0 obj"` — re-running the same mutation against this stronger assertion correctly went red. The textual round-trip is kept as a secondary check (it still proves the trailer's `/Root` reference and object body are intact) but is documented as insufficient on its own. Lesson for future increments in this package: validating offset arithmetic requires reading the xref table directly, not just confirming that *some* lookup path happens to succeed.
- **Two findings from `/advisor`'s post-implementation review, both corrected before commit.** (1) `TestShiftClassicXrefOffsets_InvalidXrefOffsetReturnsError`'s table-driven test originally shared one mutable buffer across all four subcases; `shiftClassicXrefOffsets` rewrites entries in place as it iterates, so a subcase that (under some other mutation) reached the entry-rewrite loop before erroring would leak its partial rewrite into the next subcase's input — the same order-dependence class as the map→slice fix earlier in the increment, but for shared mutable state rather than iteration order, and only half-addressed by that earlier fix. Corrected by constructing a fresh buffer per subcase. (2) The self-negating-fixture mistake recurred a third time this session (see the dedicated entry immediately above).
- **Coverage ratchet:** go_default 8833/14624 (5791 uncovered) → 8853/14627 (5774 uncovered); go_duckdb 8911/14722 (5811 uncovered) → 8931/14725 (5794 uncovered) — +20 covered, +3 denominator (the overflow guard's three new statements), net −17 uncovered on both tags, matching the per-function coverage delta exactly (17 pre-existing statements newly covered by the 19 new tests, plus 3 new statements added by the fix, all 3 covered by its own test). The exclude-filter flake (dedicated entry above, now 5 occurrences) reproduced once during this increment's `--update` run; not accepted, re-run instead. `go build ./...`, `gofmt -l`, `go vet`, and the full `go test ./...` module suite pass; `internal/pdfmeta` at 85 tests, confirmed via `grep -c "^func Test"`.
- **This fixed one instance of the bug, not the whole class — a follow-up `/advisor` consultation (after commit) caught that the ROADMAP entry's original wording overclaimed the fix's scope, and it's been corrected.** `shiftClassicXrefOffsets` is not the only site writing a fixed-width classic-xref offset field: `InjectXMPStream` (`pdfmeta.go:175,177`), `InjectOutputIntent` (`:537,539,545`), and `InjectPAdESSignature` (`:981`) all build new entries via `fmt.Fprintf(&appended, "%010d %05d n \n", off, gen)` into a growing `bytes.Buffer`. `Fprintf` doesn't truncate the way `copy()` did, but it also doesn't clamp — an offset `>= 10^10` widens the field past 10 digits, corrupting the fixed 20-byte entry width the classic xref format requires. Same root cause, same ~9.3GB reachability bound, deliberately not fixed here — see the ROADMAP entry's "Not started" pointer, which now names this as a required part of the deferred byte-surgery-cluster increment rather than leaving it implicit.

### 2026-08-06 — `internal/pdfmeta` increment 4a: 92.2% → 95.7%; the AcroForm signature-field-merging cluster, plus a second silent-corruption bug swept across the whole file instead of just the instance found

- **Covers the cluster that rewrites a PDF's `/AcroForm` dictionary to register the new signature field:** `signatureFieldReferenceRewrites`, `appendSignatureFieldToFields`, `ensureSignatureFieldFlags`, `readRefAt`, `findDictionaryEnd`, `findArrayEnd`. Selected ahead of `InjectPAdESSignature`'s own 15 uncovered blocks (deferred to increment 4b) because this cluster does the actual dictionary rewriting for signature registration — a bug here corrupts the signed PDF's form structure. All six now 100%. 31 new tests, package 85 → 116. Full masking-risk and mutation narrative lives in the ROADMAP entry of the same date; this entry covers the bug and the "sweep vs. disclose" decision a coverage-percentage summary wouldn't show.
- **A second silent-corruption bug, same class as increment 3a's `copy()` truncation but with a far lower reachability bar, flagged by `/advisor`'s pre-implementation review.** `ensureSignatureFieldFlags` parsed `/SigFlags`'s digit run via a bare `flags = flags*10 + int(c-'0')` loop, no bounds check. Confirmed directly: `ensureSignatureFieldFlags([]byte("<< /SigFlags 99999999999999999999 >>"))` returned `err=nil` and `"<< /SigFlags 7766279631452241919 >>"` — a silently wrapped, wrong value written straight into the signed PDF's AcroForm dict. Unlike 3a's bug (needs a ~9.3GB file), this one needs only a 20-digit number in a dictionary.
- **Swept the whole file rather than disclosing-and-deferring the siblings — the opposite call from 3a's `%010d` follow-up, made deliberately, not by default.** A first pass by reading found 3 accumulation sites (`readRefAt` id/gen, `readDictRef` id/gen). `/advisor` pushed back on "found by reading" specifically, given the prior cycle's incomplete disclosure — `grep -n "10 + int("` surfaced 8 sites across 6 functions (`findLastStartxref`, `readDictInt`, `readDictRef`, `parsePositiveDecimal`, `ensureSignatureFieldFlags`, `readRefAt`), 5 of them outside this increment's declared scope and already 100%-covered from increments 1/2a/3a. Extracted a shared `parseDigits(digits []byte) (int, error)` helper (`n > (math.MaxInt-d)/10` check before each multiply/add) and routed all 8 sites through it, adding one new overflow test per already-covered function to hold their coverage at 100%. The distinction from 3a's deferral: every one of these 8 sites needed the *identical* fix (3a's siblings needed a different guard — field-width overflow in `Fprintf`, not `copy()` truncation), and the helper was being written in this commit regardless of scope — leaving raw unguarded loops next to `parseDigits` in the same file would read as "this file is now uniformly safe" when it wasn't, reproducing the exact incomplete-sweep mistake the 3a follow-up commit (`02c797e`) had just finished correcting one cycle earlier.
- **Mechanical enumeration over reading, confirmed as the right call after a related miss last increment.** 3a fixed one bug (`shiftClassicXrefOffsets`'s `copy()` truncation) and only learned post-commit, via a follow-up `/advisor` review, that 3 other functions (6 `%010d`-via-`Fprintf` call sites) share the same root-cause class with a different mechanism — disclosed and deferred, not swept, since each needed its own fix. This increment's first pass repeated the same shape of miss on a fresh instance: reading found 3 of the 8 `parseDigits`-eligible accumulation sites before `/advisor` asked for a mechanical enumeration instead — `grep -n "10 + int("` found all 8 in one pass, and all 8 needed the identical fix, so they were swept immediately rather than disclosed. Worth internalizing as a standing step for any future "is this bug class fully closed" question in this file: grep the exact syntactic pattern before answering, don't just read the functions already in scope — verified again in this same increment via `grep -n '\*10\|strconv\.'` returning only `parseDigits`'s own line after the sweep, confirming no site was missed a second time.
- **Test-design note flagged by `/advisor`'s post-implementation review:** the two "insert fresh key when absent" branches (`appendSignatureFieldToFields`'s `/Fields`, `ensureSignatureFieldFlags`'s `/SigFlags`) were each tested in isolation, but their *sequencing* when both are absent was untested. `mergeSignatureFieldIntoAcroForm(<< >>, id)` runs `appendSignatureFieldToFields` first (inserts `/Fields` right after `<<`), then `ensureSignatureFieldFlags` on that already-rebuilt body (inserts `/SigFlags` right after the *same* `<<`) — so the real output is `<<\n/SigFlags 3\n/Fields [ N 0 R ] >>`, `/SigFlags` before `/Fields`. A `bytes.Count(...) == 1` presence check on each key wouldn't catch a mutation to that ordering; only an exact-byte-sequence assertion would. Added `TestMergeSignatureFieldIntoAcroForm_InsertsBothWhenBothAbsent` to close the gap.
- **A pre-existing, low-severity parsing limitation documented, not fixed:** `signatureFieldReferenceRewrites` and its callees locate keys and dictionary/array boundaries via plain byte search, not a real tokenizer — a `/Title` value containing the literal text `/AcroForm` inside parentheses could in principle confuse the scan. Traced all four `InjectPAdESSignature` call sites (`internal/signing/pades.go` → `app_documents.go`, `internal/documents/charter.go`, `internal/export/pdf.go`) and confirmed every one signs a PDF this app generated itself; user-entered text lands in content streams, not the Catalog/AcroForm dictionaries these functions scan — not reachable through this app's own generation path today.
- **Coverage ratchet, predicted before `--update` and matched exactly:** a `git stash`-isolated clean before/after package comparison (718/779 → 757/791, +39 covered/+12 total) predicted the module-wide delta; the actual ratchet run matched on both tags: go_default 8853/14627 (5774 uncovered) → 8892/14639 (5747 uncovered); go_duckdb 8931/14725 (5794 uncovered) → 8970/14737 (5767 uncovered). The exclude-filter flake (dedicated entry above, now 6 occurrences) reproduced once during this increment's `--update` run; not accepted, re-run instead. `go build ./...`, `gofmt -l`, `go vet`, `go test ./internal/pdfmeta/... -race`, and the full `go test ./...` module suite pass; `internal/pdfmeta` at 116 tests, confirmed via `grep -c "^func Test"`.

## 10. Quick map: "where do I add ..."

| Task                                      | File(s) to touch                                                          |
| ----------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| New chart kind                            | `internal/charts/registry.go` (Definition entry); pick or add engine pkg; engines.go switch; new Svelte editor; App.svelte route; Dashboard card. |
| New document kind                         | `internal/documents/registry.go` (Kind const + Definition in templates.go). Frontend create path is automatic: Dashboard fetches `ListDocumentKinds()` and renders a button per kind; the `documents` route in `App.svelte` already points to `CharterEditor` which handles any kind generically. |
| New document bespoke PDF renderer         | `internal/documents/<kind>.go` with `Render<Kind>PDF()`; switch in `documents.Render()`. |
| New database column                       | `internal/db/sqlite.go` Migrate() — additive only.                        |
| New CLI flag                              | `internal/cli/parser.go` Config struct + flag.*Var; handle in main.go.    |
| New Wails-exposed App method              | Add to `*App` in the root `main.go`; declare in `frontend/src/wails-window.d.ts`. |
| New shared editor pattern                 | `frontend/src/lib/components/charts/_*_shell.svelte` (snippet-based).     |
| Change SPDX license for a directory       | Update each file's header; add the SPDX ID to `LICENSES.md`.              |
